## Current 100% Recheck Checklist - 2026-08-01

> This checklist is the durable source of truth across context resets. Any newly discovered Settings, prompt, update or long-task defect must be added here before it is fixed; unchecked items forbid a 100% claim.

> **100% definition:** source tests alone are not completion. `100%` requires the exact installed bytes, real visible controls, one-window/native-Codex isolation, current upstream versions, cross-provider prompt behavior and the current autonomous-task artifacts to pass independently. Earlier narrower `100%` statements are permanently superseded by this checklist.

Prompt and native-Settings acceptance debt that must survive every context reset:

- [ ] Build one installed-runtime prompt ledger for Gemini, Claude and direct third-party that distinguishes: user `AGENTS.md`, personality/writing style, task-working method, editable Codex system/developer text, protected tool/protocol/permission compatibility, Anti-Codex add-on, and the provider-owned hidden base. Prove every locally added layer appears at most once and no outer development handbook leaks into the product.
- [ ] In `管理 -> 提示词`, expose every locally controllable textual layer with source, default, effective value, enabled state, edit/disable and `恢复默认`; keep only execution-critical tool/protocol/permission layers protected. Antigravity's own hidden base must be shown as opaque/read-only because its exact text is not locally available.
- [ ] Prove the native Personalization custom instruction is genuinely effective and restorable through both the native Settings page and Manager, without spending model traffic merely to monitor it; then verify its actual request-boundary effect once in each provider family during the authorized route acceptance round.
- [ ] Physically classify every native Codex Settings control as effective, Anti-Codex-managed/read-only, external to the current provider, conditional, or unsupported. Unsupported/misleading controls must be hidden or disabled with a visible reason; a control that merely stores a value but has no product effect does not pass.
- [ ] Reaccept every embedded and fallback Manager button with real mouse input and real terminal feedback. Update checks must report official Codex and CodexPlusPlus source/active versions plus whether action is needed; sync must report progress, final result and actionable failure without a stray console window.
- [ ] Reaccept current Gemini -> Claude -> saved-third-party routing, current-hash autonomous-task output and same-task tool evidence. The host may give outcome-only feedback or exact `继续`, never implementation steps or micro-stage planning.

- [x] Make update checking, synchronization handoff, rollback history and original failure results truthful; committed as `6c9dcad5` with focused offline regression green.
- [x] Classify every visible native Settings control as effective, managed/read-only, external or conditional; the source/ASAR truth matrix is recorded in `findings.md` and physical acceptance remains below.
- [x] Inject Anti-Codex-only session flags for fixed `never` approval and `danger-full-access` sandbox so the native controls become genuinely managed/read-only; do not use machine-wide policy and do not present network as an independent switch in full-access mode. Source patch, current-ASAR copy proof and fail-closed tests are green; installed physical proof remains in the shared activation item below.
- [x] Hide only the misleading ChatGPT Account and web Security links while preserving the local Codex Security conditional page and every real local Settings section. Current-ASAR copy proof removes only those two external entries and preserves local Agent/plugin entries.
- [x] Prove offline that isolated `AGENTS.md` is a separate model-visible user item and is not structurally coupled to Codex developer text.
- [x] Move complete editable Codex system/developer prompt policy to the single CodexPlusPlus request boundary for Gemini, Claude and direct third-party routes; preserve user messages, isolated `AGENTS.md`, tool schemas and required compatibility guidance. Eleven-patch `v1.2.44` candidate is built and hash-pinned but not activated.
- [x] Remove duplicate Antigravity-side policy injection, expose the complete observed/default/effective prompt with edit, disable and restore-default controls, and disclose that provider-hidden prompts are outside local control. Source UI and real-mouse contracts are green; installed physical proof remains below.
- [x] Add focused Responses/Chat, reserved/non-reserved route, default/custom/disabled, snapshot, native custom-instruction and no-double-injection tests. Prompt/provider coverage passes `68/68`; component/supervisor/security/candidate coverage passes `101/101`.
- [x] Run the complete no-traffic clean-source preflight plus one independent code review; record exact failures instead of claiming completion. Clean commit `8c8af90` passes `908/908` with `usedRealTraffic:false`, zero failure/skip/warning; the review's snapshot-disclosure concern was fixed and its full-access concern was confirmed as the pre-existing copied-shell-only policy.
- [x] Repair the exact windowless-root shortcut failure without restarting or interrupting the native task: invoke the copied shell's existing single-instance window recreation with the isolated user-data identity, fail closed if the handoff does not exit, and prove the same root PID/window/native-Codex identity in a live idle-task round.

- [x] When the current task/window was safely idle, perform one guarded activation only; transaction `20260802T052530941Z-bd552bc3a129442b9ff9371ac18feaa5` restored one visible ready Anti-Codex and did not alter native Codex or create a second window.
- [ ] Physically exercise the installed Manager update buttons, prompt page, native Personalization and the full supported Settings/button matrix using real mouse input; verify visible terminal results and one-window stability.
- [ ] Recheck the autonomous long-task's current `a27038fe...` artifacts and same-task tool evidence against the final media/package gates; do not reuse the superseded `1d5b...` acceptance, and resume only from an observed defect with result feedback or `继续` under the established provider priority.

The red desktop toast means this Antigravity/proxy path is unstable, not that the Codex-owned tool path is broken.

## Goal
Finish Anti-Codex as a permanent single-account native-Codex-parity product. The product is complete only when all four requirements hold: a guarded complete Codex desktop client copy, Antigravity model text behind Codex-owned tools and task state, host-verified real long-task results, and a native-Codex-like visible multi-turn agentic experience. Account separation and worker MCP remain optional development appendices, not product requirements.

## Client Sync Contract
- Anti-Codex client behavior must track the current native Codex client by guarded copy/sync. This is a client-parity requirement, not a feature-by-feature rebuild plan.
- When native Codex updates, the preferred maintenance path is to sync the updated Codex host/client surface, then reapply Anti-Codex branding, isolation, launcher, and model-adapter patches.
- If a native Codex feature is missing or broken in Anti-Codex after sync, treat it as a sync/parity bug first. Do not rebuild that feature through Antigravity Desktop/coworker unless the user explicitly approves a different architecture.

## Baseline Principles
- Do not modify or depend on the running state of the original Codex installation.
- Treat Codex app-server behavior and schemas as the client contract.
- Treat Antigravity Desktop/CLI discovery as a model adapter boundary, not as a coworker, tool executor, or UI automation layer.
- Product direction: Anti-Codex is a guarded copy/sync of the Codex client plus Anti-Codex isolation, branding, and an Antigravity-entitlement model adapter. When Codex updates, Anti-Codex should update by guarded host sync from that Codex client surface, not by reimplementing native Codex features one by one.
- Prefer carrying forward native Codex client/tool behavior instead of rebuilding tool features in the Antigravity adapter. Host sync should be treated as a guarded Codex client copy/incremental patch flow plus Anti-Codex branding, isolation, and model-adapter changes.
- Do not replace missing Codex-client behavior with Antigravity coworker/Desktop behavior. If a Codex capability is absent after sync, treat it as a host-sync/client-parity bug unless the user explicitly accepts a different architecture.
- Do not run Antigravity coworker/native tool-bridge traffic as part of default testing or release gates; the default product path is still Codex-owned tools plus Antigravity model text.
- Optional account-isolation scripts may remain for development experiments, but they must not enter the product action sequence, Manager daily actions, release requirements, or goal blockers.
- Worker MCP is useful for development delegation and evidence only. Its transport health must not redefine the Anti-Codex product path; Xiaoqiang 1/2/3/4 remain archived and must not be scheduled.
- Preserve the native Codex event, tool, MCP, skill, cancellation, compaction, and session semantics; a visual shell is not an acceptable deliverable.
- Model names and IDs must come from the active Antigravity account and must be provably used by the execution layer.
- No feature is complete until installed-build validation passes.
- Do not restart the user's original Codex during development or validation.

## Phases

### Phase 1: Protocol and Architecture Audit
Status: completed_for_v1_transport
- Inventory every endpoint and transport used by Codex Desktop.
- Render the model catalog through the bundled Codex CLI.
- Identify reconnect causes from app-server and gateway logs.
- Compare Antigravity Desktop HTTP service, CLI, and persistent-process options.
- Decide the supported v1 architecture and explicit unsupported surfaces.
- Current v1 transport direction: model-only Antigravity transport. CLI is the stability default when available; desktop/Cascade may be used only as a model-text channel with Antigravity tools/coworker behavior disabled.

### Phase 2: Stable Gateway Core
Status: mostly_complete_for_v1
- Split lifecycle, protocol projection, model catalog, and Antigravity transport responsibilities.

- Keep the model-only route as default and verify it through installed Anti-Codex startup and result-based desktop tasks.

### Phase 4: Failure Recovery and Isolation
Status: mostly_complete_for_v1
- Recover from Antigravity Desktop restart, account switch, proxy interruption, and CLI failure.
- Prevent duplicate launchers, translators, and orphan processes.
- Keep original Codex files, processes, state, ports, and shortcuts isolated.
- Add bounded retries, circuit breaking, and useful local diagnostics.
- Completed for current bridge: translator-only recovery for an already-open Anti-Codex window, avoiding desktop process kill/restart and avoiding the shared launcher mutex.
- Completed for current bridge: bounded Anti-Codex main-process restart, translator watchdog recovery, and Antigravity Desktop watchdog recovery.
- Antigravity Desktop recovery now adopts an already-running supported desktop process, resolves installed candidates from Windows uninstall metadata, matches the exact executable path, requires a stable process window, and applies a 60-second failure cooldown.
- Installed failure drill passed: Antigravity Desktop restarted from PID 22128 to PID 34764 while Anti-Codex stayed on PID 17428, followed by a successful real model response.
- Antigravity local-service discovery is cached inside the translator and refreshed after connection failure, avoiding a blocking Windows process scan on every readiness check and model turn.
- Translator watchdog health checks now allow three bounded failures before restart, preventing a busy but healthy gateway from being mistaken for a crashed process.
- Account/model refresh no longer requires closing Anti-Codex in the healthy path. The translator exposes `/anticodex/models/refresh`, the installed shortcut calls it first, and the restart-based refresh remains only as fallback.
- Antigravity account-backed requests now pass through one shared FIFO queue across chat completions, compact/non-streaming Responses, and streamed Responses. Queued streamed requests can be cancelled before entering upstream, preventing overlapping Cascade turns and reducing reconnect/stale-session risk.
- Installed compaction/session-resume verification now proves readable context compaction, tool suppression during compact mode, direct compaction-item replay, and persisted response-ID continuation. Compaction-state recall no longer trips the fake-tool-claim guard, while genuine future tool promises remain blocked.
- Installed overall now includes a fast mandatory streaming-cancellation contract check with matching response IDs, cancelled terminal status, no completed/failed terminal event, and healthy post-cancel readiness.
- Repeated proxy/network failures now open a short Antigravity failure circuit. New requests fail fast with retryable metadata instead of piling into the Cascade queue, while `/health.ok` remains process-healthy so the launcher does not restart the translator for account/proxy readiness failures.
- Re-launching an already-open installed Anti-Codex now resolves the real install root from `runtime\scripts`, detects the existing main window, activates it, and exits quickly instead of falling into full startup monitoring.
- Normal startup now reuses the last successful Antigravity model catalog when available, avoiding blocking live model discovery on every launch. Explicit account/model refresh remains available through the shortcut and `ANTICODEX_FORCE_STARTUP_MODEL_REFRESH=1`.
- Cold relaunch is now covered by an installed verifier that stops only Anti-Codex host/launcher/translator processes, restarts through the installed launcher, and checks original Codex isolation.
- Account switching now has a privacy-preserving fingerprint proof through `/anticodex/account`; the installed translator hot-refreshes models when the comparable Antigravity account identity changes.
- Installed branding isolation now covers the executable name, visible window title, patched Electron app name, tray tooltip source, and Windows app user model ID, so the lower-right/taskbar identity no longer falls back to Codex.
- Installed external branding verification now checks the live Windows process name, window title, and executable metadata, so a future Codex sync cannot silently expose the main Anti-Codex entry point as `Codex`.
- Local plugin/connector projection now prefers Anti-Codex's own config home before the shared original Codex home, while preserving richer shared metadata for duplicate plugin records.
- Skills, connector directory metadata, and local app-tool cache snapshots have moved from live shared-home dependency to Anti-Codex-owned local copies. This reduces dependency on the original Codex home while keeping only larger plugin host sidecars on the current staged import/link path.
- Translator-only recovery now refreshes those local snapshots before starting the gateway, so MCP/skills and connector metadata recover with the gateway rather than waiting for a full desktop relaunch.
- Antigravity-mode startup now treats the original Codex config as import-only: shared MCP/config blocks may be copied into Anti-Codex's own config, but plugin registration restoration and permission defaults no longer write back to the original global Codex config.

## Completion Criteria
- No recurring "reconnecting" state during normal idle or use.
- Installed Anti-Codex shows the current account's exact model names.
- A selected model ID is visible in gateway logs and reaches Antigravity unchanged.
- Tool calls, MCP calls, skills, cancellation, compaction, session resume, and streaming use native Codex contracts rather than prompt simulation.
- Any capability that cannot be implemented natively is visibly disabled and documented, not silently faked.
- Original Codex remains usable and unchanged.
- All protocol tests pass and the installed application passes failure-recovery checks.

## Errors Encountered
- 2026-06-20: the first UI-state test patch assumed an inline `const managerPage` declaration; the file declares it earlier and assigns later. The patch was rejected with no partial edit, then split against the exact assignment site.
- 2026-06-20: a combined PowerShell/regex source search failed because an embedded quote terminated the command string. No files changed; subsequent searches use separate simple patterns instead of one quoted alternation.
- Responses websocket native transport remains intentionally disabled; the installed client is configured to use HTTP and currently shows no reconnect loop. Real WebSocket Upgrade requests are now explicitly rejected with verified `501 unsupported_websocket` instead of falling through default socket handling.
- Remote plugin marketplace support remains disabled; local skills and MCP inheritance are available.
- Remote app connector linking is not yet available without ChatGPT account flow; cached connector directory entries can be displayed/graded, but `isAccessible:false` means they must not be advertised as already usable.
- Historical boundary resolved again: verified native MCP was demoted from default to opt-in because Antigravity's native permission UI is hostile to the intended Anti-Codex experience. The Codex-owned prompt-mediated bridge is the stable default until the native event protocol can be used without Antigravity approval prompts.
- Antigravity's public Python SDK cannot become the primary transport because it requires a Gemini API key or Vertex credentials instead of using the signed-in Antigravity account allowance.
- Desktop Cascade exposes internal structured tool types, but arbitrary custom tool definitions are gated to SDK executables and are not accepted from the ordinary account-backed Cascade client path.
- Antigravity CLI/SQLite is now an accepted stability path for model text. Desktop Cascade is not the default coworker path; if used, it must stay model-only with Anti-Codex owning tools and task execution.
- Actual account switching and a real external proxy-off drill require the user's signed-in account/proxy controls; the one-click refresh path, transient proxy/network recovery behavior, and installed account/proxy diagnostic evidence collector are implemented.
- The circuit breaker covers repeated transient failures, and the account/proxy readiness verifier now classifies the observed failure type; a real proxy-off/on drill is still the only way to observe the exact errors produced by the user's proxy stack.
- Antigravity CLI worker and DeepSeek worker remain useful for narrow assistance, but their edits must be treated as untrusted; this run had one Antigravity worker failure and one DeepSeek scope overrun that required main-agent cleanup/review.
- Added `test-anticodex-sync-readiness.ps1` as a read-only installed sync readiness verifier. Checks sync entry, host candidate validator, active/rollback markers, maintenance marker absence, and `Anti-Codex.exe` identity without executing sync or modifying the host. Included in installed overall check. Removed duplicate `$checks.toolBridgeMode` line.
- Added `test-anticodex-mcp-tool-loop.ps1` as an installed MCP namespace tool-loop verifier. Uses namespace `mcp__node_repl__` + subtool `js`, verifies reverse-mapping of flattened `mcp__node_repl__js` back to `js` in `function_call`, and proves `function_call_output` continuation without duplicate invocation. No real MCP tools are executed; only Responses protocol output is inspected. Included in installed overall check as `$checks.mcpToolLoop`. Static regression added to launcher test suite.
- Added `test-anticodex-custom-tool-loop.ps1` as an installed custom/freeform tool-loop verifier. Uses `apply_patch` custom tool with `type="custom"` and grammar metadata (`syntax="lark"`), verifies bridge returns `custom_tool_call` with name=apply_patch and input equal to the full patch body (with `*** Begin Patch`/`*** End Patch` markers), and proves `custom_tool_call_output` continuation without duplicate invocation. No real patches are executed; only Responses protocol output is inspected. Included in installed overall check as `$checks.customToolLoop`. Static regression added to launcher test suite. Latest installed overall passed with `customToolLoop.ok:true`.
- Model display names now preserve exact model/version numbers and use explicit public model-ID reasoning suffixes as the authority. The catalog, native Cascade resolver, and route audits all normalize mismatched names. Installed full-model routing now fails on reasoning-label drift and passed all eight visible models after deployment.
- Installed overall now includes non-streaming and streaming parallel multi-tool loop verifiers. Both require exactly two calls, unique call IDs, preserved arguments, reverse-order result continuation, no duplicate calls, and no prompt-protocol leakage. The streaming verifier additionally requires SSE output-item calls to match the completed response by name and call ID.

## 2026-06-20 Native Bridge Restart Inheritance
- Root cause: the running desktop monitor was created while the native bridge default was temporarily `0`. Later translator-only watchdog restarts inherited that stale process value even after source and installed files were updated to default to `1`.
- Fixed: the normal Anti-Codex desktop launcher, Manager gateway-only recovery, and translator recovery verifier now explicitly set `ANTICODEX_NATIVE_TOOL_BRIDGE=1` before starting the translator.
- Regression: launcher coverage now requires all three formal entry paths to preserve the native bridge. The focused launcher suite passed 75/75.
- Installed verification: the old monitor was fully replaced. Diagnostics now reports `native-mcp-same-cascade`, native bridge enabled, native Antigravity tool events enabled, and eight models. UI state, tool-bridge mode, fast sync readiness, and fast installed overall all passed; the final fast overall completed in about 21.7 seconds with no failures.

## 2026-06-20 Manager Codex-Style Redesign

## 2026-06-20 Translator Recovery Verifier Timeout Fix
- Fixed: the translator recovery verifier no longer relies on Windows PowerShell 5.1 `WaitForExit(ms)` for bounded child waits. On this host that call did not return at the requested millisecond deadline, making a healthy recovery look frozen.
- Fixed: the verifier redirects translator-only launcher stdout/stderr to short temp logs and uses `HasExited` polling with a 12-second launcher cap plus a 25-second overall budget.
- Completed: the verifier now reports `totalElapsedMs` and per-phase timings (`launcherElapsedMs`, `healthElapsedMs`, `pluginsElapsedMs`, `whamAppsElapsedMs`) so future stalls show the exact phase.
- Installed verification: the deployed runtime recovery verifier passed in about 9.4 seconds with `accountReady:true`, `modelCount:8`, and `originalCodexConfigUnchanged:true`.
- Installed overall verification: fast no-traffic overall passed in about 24.1 seconds with Manager `native-panel-v63`, eight exact models, no ChatGPT login/reconnect text, and one Anti-Codex translator.

## 2026-06-20 Child Process Timeout Hardening
- Fixed: bounded child-process waits in `start-deepcodex.ps1`, `manage-anticodex.ps1`, `sync-anticodex.ps1`, and `test-anticodex-installed-overall.ps1` now use `HasExited` polling instead of Windows PowerShell 5.1 `WaitForExit(timeout)`.
- Fixed: `manage-anticodex.ps1` is stored as UTF-8 with BOM because it is executed directly by Windows PowerShell 5.1 and contains Chinese UI strings. This prevents the Start Menu manager from parsing mojibake as broken PowerShell syntax.
- Regression: launcher tests now reject the old timeout waits, require the helper polling functions, and require the manager script BOM.
- Verification: full launcher regression passed 75/75; parser checks passed for the updated scripts; installed fast no-traffic overall passed; installed `manage-anticodex.ps1 -Action Status` returned Chinese status with eight models.
- Audit note: Antigravity's narrow documentation task reported exit code 2 but still produced the expected single-file audit at `docs/audits/child-process-timeout-hardening-2026-06-20.md`; the file was reviewed and kept.

## 2026-06-20 Third-Party Upstream V1 Hardening
- Completed: Anti-Codex now supports an optional third-party upstream profile alongside the default Antigravity path. OpenAI-compatible and Anthropic profiles are normalized through `translator/upstream-provider.mjs`; API keys are stored separately through Windows DPAPI and are never written into the JSON profile.
- Completed: switching provider/model preserves the same Anti-Codex state root, CODEX_HOME, project list, and chat history. Provider changes restart only the local model gateway path and do not switch or clear the desktop/session data directory.

Basic chat failure blocks saving; streaming/tool-call gaps are warnings so compatible chat-only providers can still be saved with visible limitations.
- Hardened: saving a third-party profile no longer trusts client-supplied stage results. The server reruns the connection test before saving and stores sanitized connection-stage evidence in the profile capabilities.
- Completed: `/health` now exposes a redacted `upstreamProfile` summary for third-party mode, including protocol/model and saved connection capability evidence. The embedded Manager status card shows that evidence instead of only saying the API is configured.
- Verification: `node --check` passed for `translator/adaptive-server.mjs`, `translator/upstream-provider.mjs`, and `scripts/anticodex-manager-inject.js`; focused regression passed 146/146 across upstream provider, upstream integration, and launcher coverage; PowerShell upstream profile storage tests passed.
- Worker note: two Antigravity CLI attempts for the small Manager invalidation task failed or lost their job result. A later DeepSeek review job incorrectly edited the file despite a read-only task; its final file state was reviewed and covered by the 146-test regression. Future worker assignments for this area should use explicit edit/no-edit enforcement and review diffs before accepting any output.

- Final acceptance requirement: before claiming Anti-Codex is 100% complete, run a full-system usability pass after all scripted gates are green. This must include at least one or two small real coding projects executed end-to-end through Anti-Codex itself, proving it can plan, edit files, use local tools/MCP/skills/app-tool surfaces as available, verify, and finish without using ChatGPT login, fake connector authorization, or mutating native Codex state.
- Current usability priority: Anti-Codex must be treated as usable only when the actual desktop can answer, write/run a small local tool, complete a file-plus-MCP work item, and finish at least one small real coding project. The current real evidence refreshed on 2026-06-27 passes desktop UI chat, real mini-tool, real work, and the new real small-project gate.
- Current small-project evidence: `test-anticodex-real-small-project.ps1 -AllowRealTraffic` generated `package.json`, `word-tally.mjs`, and `word-tally.test.mjs`, ran `node --test`, ran the CLI, and received a Chinese final confirmation in about 28 seconds. The verifier now forces the first tool call and clears a stale Antigravity session lock on failure when diagnostics show no active upstream request.
- Desktop UI small-project evidence: `test-anticodex-ui-chat-smoke.mjs --task-mode local-project` now sends a file-writing task through the Anti-Codex desktop UI, verifies `index.html`, `README.md`, `done.txt`, exact marker text, and idle diagnostics, and writes `latest-ui-local-project-smoke.json`. Latest run on 2026-06-27 passed in about 138 seconds, proving desktop task completion while leaving latency as the next usability concern.
- Historical 100% blocker snapshot, superseded by the final usability acceptance update below: sync-readiness reported `source_codex_changed` because native Codex advanced while the active Anti-Codex host remained older; fixing that required explicit-approval host sync. Full no-traffic overall then reached UI-state but failed on diagnostics/sync-readiness for that same update-status drift, and post-verification was stale.
- P0 usability gate: keep `ui-web-search-smoke` as a release-grade desktop test. Latest refreshed run on 2026-06-27 passed in about 22 seconds.

The real UI free-chat smoke for `GBT5.6什么时候发布啊` now passes in about 12.6 seconds, returns web-sourced answer text, and leaves the Antigravity session lock inactive.

- Desktop chat regression on 2026-06-27: reproduced the user-visible "simple message keeps thinking / repeats old task" failure. Exact-reply prompts after the report-site task were repeatedly swallowed by the old report-site context and answered with `UI_REPORT_SITE_REAL_1782536200`. Fixes now clear stale native continuations when a plain new user message follows old tool output, append a latest-user focus block to Antigravity prompts, and direct-handle explicit "Reply with exactly X and nothing else" prompts in the gateway.
- Latest desktop usability evidence after the UI-send lock fix: `test-anticodex-ui-chat-smoke.mjs --marker UI_BASIC_REPLY_LOCKED_1782546600` passed in about 14.5 seconds; a non-exact normal Chinese chat prompt passed in about 22.7 seconds with a real queued Antigravity request and idle diagnostics; `--task-mode web-search --marker UI_WEB_SEARCH_LOCKED_1782547000` passed in about 22.5 seconds with Chinese source URLs; `--task-mode coding-project --marker UI_CODING_PROJECT_LOCKED_1782547800` passed in about 51.4 seconds with file-level evidence and passing `node:test`/CLI output.
- Immediate usability status: Anti-Codex desktop is usable again for normal chat, web-search answers, a runnable coding project, a small file-writing project, and a research-to-report/site task from the desktop UI. The key regression found and fixed in the verifier was not just model routing: parallel UI smoke runs and a broad send-button selector could leave the desktop in a stale stop/queued state while backend diagnostics were idle.

## 2026-07-13 Architecture Baseline And Progress Correction

- `docs/PRODUCT_ARCHITECTURE.md` is now the permanent, authoritative product architecture. Anti-Codex remains a Codex Desktop client copy; Codex alone owns task control, tools, continuation, permissions, cancellation, compaction, and local execution. The local gateway may perform only authentication, model discovery, and structural protocol conversion.
- CC Switch was evaluated as the preferred source/reference for Codex provider proxy behavior. Its full desktop product does not provide the required Antigravity entitlement bridge, separate branded Codex client, or isolated Anti-Codex runtime, so it does not replace the whole project. Its MIT-licensed Codex transform, streaming, SSE, and tool-history modules should replace locally invented protocol behavior where practical.
- The current codebase is not restarting from zero. Client isolation/branding/update sync, Antigravity account/model/text transport, Manager, installation, diagnostics, runtime fingerprints, and result-oriented verifier infrastructure are retained.
- The active translator has drifted beyond the architecture boundary: it contains internal tool injection, command synthesis, patch repair, forced verification, task-specific behavior, retry/continuation control, and business completion heuristics. These are now explicitly scheduled for removal or default-disable from the production path.
- Product completion is reset to 64/100 based on weighted user-visible capability, not code volume or old green reports. Infrastructure is roughly 85-90% complete, but strict tool protocol and long-task reliability carry high weight and are not complete.
- Earlier `goalComplete:true` and all-green release statements are superseded as final product evidence. A user-reported simple BAT task failed and repeated launches, and the latest four-turn inventory journey produced zero project file changes across initial/repair attempts. Future completion requires native-path file, command, test, process, and multi-turn evidence without translator semantic compensation.

## 2026-07-13 Structured Upstream Root Cause And Durable Direction Ledger

- The first real Codex Desktop simple task on the native handler failed after 289872 ms. Codex submitted normally, routed to `gemini-3.5-flash-low`, executed real local tools, and returned tool results, but the requested files were not created and the model eventually asked for a task that was still present in history.
- A proposed bare `[ASSISTANT]` suffix passed 26 focused tests but made the real rerun loop on `Get-ChildItem` and `get_goal`; the run was cancelled, the patch was reverted, and the runtime was restored. Prompt-shape tests are not continuation evidence.
- Direct Cascade trajectories proved the production defect: prompts were 108942 to 143373 characters, reported model input stayed near 17226 tokens, and model thinking explicitly rejected `[CODEX_PROVIDER_PROTOCOL]`, `[CODEX_TOOLS]`, and XML tool blocks as prompt injection. Raw tool output can also contain the same role delimiters and corrupt framing.
- The production path must therefore stop flattening system/developer/user/tool roles and tool schemas into one user message. The preferred replacement is a structured model interface using the existing Antigravity entitlement; native MCP/same-Cascade continuation is a fallback only after proving no permission popup and Codex-owned execution.
- `docs/PRODUCT_ARCHITECTURE.md` and `docs/CURRENT_IMPLEMENTATION_AUDIT.md` remain the two authoritative project ledgers. They now define replacement-first migration, the exact keep/improve/delete/incomplete lists, the 64% product score, and the eight-step dependency order. Older `goalComplete:true` handoffs are superseded.
- Current maintenance evidence is also recorded: 79 Anti-Codex JSONL session files (21.36 MB), 8 response-history recovery points, 33 backup directories, and 83 extra registered historical worker/detached worktrees. These are cleanup candidates after backup/dirty/unmerged checks, not product architecture.
- Fixed order: structured upstream contract; normal fail-closed route; real desktop simple loop; true streaming/history; compact/WebSocket; long-task edge matrix; remove unreachable semantic code/tests; history/worktree/UI/release closeout.

## 2026-07-16 Three-Layer Long-Task Root-Cause Repair

- Architecture remains locked: Layer 1 is the isolated official Codex shell/harness, Layer 2 is pinned upstream CodexPlusPlus, and Layer 3 is the thin Antigravity Chat-to-Cascade adapter. No fourth task controller was added and no CodexPlusPlus provider/protocol behavior was changed.
- Layer 3 repair: native Codex task-title requests now preserve their standard JSON schema as a structured-output contract and are forced to text-only mode. The title helper cannot receive Codex tools, select a tool or run the embedded user task. Same-process duplicate tool-result continuations now share one Cascade resume, replay its raw events, reject conflicting results and keep a bounded completed replay tombstone. A consecutive tool chain can replace its claimed slot even with `maxSessions=1` without cancelling or restarting itself.
- Layer 1 repair: Desktop submission now performs one real CDP mouse press/release, then checks marker-scoped native rollout/request evidence and composer state before any Enter fallback. Synthetic DOM submission and automatic fresh-task replay were removed. Formal long-task turns require an eight-second native-request quiet window and reject a title thread that uses tools/repeats requests or any unexpected thread that completes a tool call.
- Layer 1 lifecycle repair: the 14:37 apparent client crash was a supervisor false shutdown, not a Windows application crash. One `Get-CimInstance` child timed out with `spawnSync powershell.exe ETIMEDOUT`; the ready supervisor mislabeled it `startup_failed` and killed its owned client. Ready-state supervision now checks the already-owned client PID directly and never launches PowerShell/WMI every second. Startup scans retry transient failures. Manager watcher/bridge children carry the supervisor PID and exit promptly when that owner disappears, preventing stale children from attaching after a restart.
- Gate evidence repair: terminal provider failure reports retain every completed earlier journey turn and deduplicate the terminal turn. A quota stop no longer erases valid long-task progress.
- Offline verification is green: focused architecture/submit/activity/continuation coverage 81/81, provider/server/fixed-route integration 45/45, supervisor lifecycle 11/11, and complete source preflight 340/340 with `noTraffic:true` and `usedRealTraffic:false`. - Completion accounting: all three component implementations remain at 100%; release acceptance remains 97% because the post-fix live start/idle check and Gate 8 real Antigravity long-task acceptance are still required. Layer 2 remains independently complete and unchanged. Do not raise release completion until the live result-based run proves the repaired Layer 1 and Layer 3 behavior.
- Next fixed order: finish one no-traffic runtime launch/readiness/idle check with exactly one visible client; wait for the conservative Antigravity quota boundary (approximately 18:11 +08:00 from the latest exhausted route); issue at most one fixed-model quota probe; only after a successful marker-matched probe run one locked formal Gate 8 journey.
